I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Is the feeling that you need to keep checking the car mostly because you're afraid something bad will happen, or mostly because you feel anxious if you don't do it?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I just said, I'm not really sure which one it is. \n\nI just know that before I can take a ride, I have to keep checking everything. I just have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Is the relief you feel after checking the car more about feeling safe or more about feeling less anxious?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know how else to answer this. I don't sit there and analyze how I feel after. \n\nIt just means I can finally put the car in gear. That's it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check the car repeatedly if you didn't feel anxious about missing something?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I've never really... not felt that way. So I couldn't tell you. \n\nI just know I have to check. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you're checking the car to make sure everything is okay, or to make yourself feel better?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really see the difference, honestly. \n\nI just check it so I can start my shift. I'm not really paying attention to whether it's for the car or... or for me. It's just what I have to do.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Before I can take a ride, I... I end up walking around the car. A lot. Just... checking the tires, the lights, the doors. The meter, my paperwork. I just keep doing it. Over and over. \n\nSometimes I... um... I take pictures of them on my phone. And if it doesn't feel right, I... I have to restart the whole check. It's... it's embarrassing, honestly. Because I'll do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me, and I'm already late. I just... I have to do it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel less anxious after you've finished walking around the car and checking everything?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. A little, I guess. But... it doesn't really last long.\n\nDoctor: Are you walking around the car and checking everything to try to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess so. Like I was saying, that's why I keep doing all that... checking the tires and the lights and taking the pictures. Even with the passengers standing right there. I just... I don't want to miss something and cause a crash or... yeah. I just have to be sure.\n\nDoctor: Is checking the car and taking pictures mostly to make yourself feel less anxious or mostly to actually prevent an accident from happening?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. Maybe... both? \n\nLike I just said, before I take a ride, I just have to keep going around checking everything. The doors, the paperwork, taking those pictures... and if it feels wrong, I have to restart the whole thing. Even if I'm late and they're waiting. I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Is the feeling that you need to keep checking the car mostly because you're afraid something bad will happen, or mostly because you feel anxious if you don't do it?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I just said, I'm not really sure which one it is. \n\nI just know that before I can take a ride, I have to keep checking everything. The tires, the lights... the doors, the meter, the paperwork. Just over and over. Taking the pictures. If it doesn't feel right, I have to restart the whole thing. Even with the passengers waiting right there and I'm already late. I just... I have to do it. So maybe it's the anxious thing. I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Is the main reason you repeat the checks to stop the anxious feeling or to actually make sure nothing bad happens?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I've told you this already. I just have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Is the relief you feel after checking the car more about feeling safe or more about feeling less anxious?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know how else to answer this. I don't sit there and analyze how I feel after. \n\nIt just means I can finally put the car in gear. That's it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check the car repeatedly if you didn't feel anxious about missing something?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I've never really... not felt that way. So I couldn't tell you. \n\nI just know I have to check. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you're checking the car to make sure everything is okay, or to make yourself feel better?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really see the difference, honestly. \n\nI just check it so I can start my shift. I'm not really paying attention to whether it's for the car or... or for me. It's just what I have to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the bad things you're afraid might happen would still worry you if you didn't have these repeating thoughts about them?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't know. The thoughts are the problem. That's... that's why I'm here. \n\nEven when I know I did the safety check on the cab, the thought just keeps hitting me... over and over... that I missed something. It just doesn't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the thoughts about missing something on the car would bother you as much if there were no consequences for missing something?\n\nPatient: I... I guess I've never really thought about it like that. \n\nThere's always consequences, though. In a car, anyway. So... I don't really know how to answer that. I just know the thoughts are there.\n\nDoctor: If you were absolutely certain that nothing bad would happen, would the thoughts about missing something on the car still bother you?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. \n\nI mean... if I knew for sure nothing bad would happen? I guess... maybe not? But I can't know that. So... I don't really know.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't really know. Maybe... both? \n\nLike I just said, before I take a ride, I just have to keep going around checking everything. The doors, the paperwork, taking those pictures... and if it feels wrong, I have to restart the whole thing. Even if I'm late and they're waiting. I do it to make sure nothing bad happens, but... yeah, doing it makes that bad feeling go away for a minute, too. I just... I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Is the feeling that you need to keep checking the car mostly because you're afraid something bad will happen, or mostly because you feel anxious if you don't do it?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I just said, I'm not really sure which one it is. \n\nI just know that before I can take a ride, I have to keep checking everything. The tires, the lights... the doors, the meter, the paperwork. Just over and over. Taking the pictures. If it doesn't feel right, I have to restart the whole thing. Even with the passengers waiting right there and I'm already late. I just... I have to do it. So maybe it's the anxious thing. I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Is the main reason you repeat the checks to stop the anxious feeling or to actually make sure nothing bad happens?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I've told you this already. It's just... I have to do it. \n\nI just... I check the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork. Over and over. I take the pictures. If it doesn't feel right, I start over. Even if people are waiting. I just... I don't know why exactly. I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Is the feeling of relief you get from checking the car mostly because you feel like you've prevented something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Look, I... I really don't know. I'm not trying to be difficult, it's just... I don't think about it like that. \n\nI just do the checks, and then I can drive. That's... that's all I know. I just have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Is the relief you feel after checking the car more about feeling safe or more about feeling less anxious?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know how else to answer this. I don't sit there and analyze how I feel after. \n\nIt just means I can finally put the car in gear. That's it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check the car repeatedly if you didn't feel anxious about missing something?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I've never really... not felt that way. So I couldn't tell you. \n\nI just know I have to check. I don't really see the difference, honestly. \n\nI just check it so I can start my shift. I'm not really paying attention to whether it's for the car or... or for me. It's just what I have to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the bad things you're afraid might happen would still worry you if you didn't have these repeating thoughts about them?\n\nPatient: I mean... It just doesn't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the thoughts about missing something on the car would bother you as much if there were no consequences for missing something?\n\nPatient: I... I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Is the feeling of relief you get from checking the car mostly because you feel like you've prevented something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Look, I... I really don't know. I'm not trying to be difficult, it's just... I don't think about it like that. \n\nI just do the checks, and then I can drive. That's... that's all I know. I don't really see the difference, honestly. \n\nI just check it so I can start my shift. I'm not really paying attention to whether it's for the car or... or for me. I don't know. The thoughts are the problem. It just doesn't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the thoughts about missing something on the car would bother you as much if there were no consequences for missing something?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Is the main reason you repeat the checks to stop the anxious feeling or to actually make sure nothing bad happens?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I've told you this already. It's just... I have to do it. \n\nI just... I check the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork. Over and over. I take the pictures. If it doesn't feel right, I start over. Even if people are waiting. I just... I don't know why exactly. I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Is the feeling of relief you get from checking the car mostly because you feel like you've prevented something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Look, I... I really don't know. I'm not trying to be difficult, it's just... I don't think about it like that. \n\nI just do the checks, and then I can drive. That's... that's all I know. I just have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Is the relief you feel after checking the car more about feeling safe or more about feeling less anxious?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know how else to answer this. I don't sit there and analyze how I feel after. \n\nIt just means I can finally put the car in gear. That's it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check the car repeatedly if you didn't feel anxious about missing something?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I've never really... not felt that way. So I couldn't tell you. \n\nI just know I have to check. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you're checking the car to make sure everything is okay, or to make yourself feel better?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really see the difference, honestly. \n\nI just check it so I can start my shift. I'm not really paying attention to whether it's for the car or... or for me. It's just what I have to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the bad things you're afraid might happen would still worry you if you didn't have these repeating thoughts about them?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't know. The thoughts are the problem. That's... that's why I'm here. \n\nEven when I know I did the safety check on the cab, the thought just keeps hitting me... over and over... that I missed something. That someone's gonna get hurt, or my boss is gonna think I lied about doing the check. \n\nI just... I can't make it shut up. Not when I'm driving, not when I'm trying to study. It just doesn't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the thoughts about missing something on the car would bother you as much if there were no consequences for missing something?\n\nPatient: I... I guess I've never really thought about it like that. \n\nThere's always consequences, though. In a car, anyway. So... I don't really know how to answer that. I just know the thoughts are there.\n\nDoctor: If you were absolutely certain that nothing bad would happen, would the thoughts about missing something on the car still bother you?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. \n\nI mean... if I knew for sure nothing bad would happen? I guess... maybe not? But I can't know that. So... I don't really know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still check the car repeatedly if you didn't feel anxious about it, but still thought something bad might happen?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. Probably not. But I do feel anxious, so... I check. \n\nI just... before I can even take a ride, I have to go around the car. The tires, the lights, the doors... the meter, the paperwork. I just keep checking them. Over and over. Sometimes I take pictures on my phone just to... prove it to myself. If it doesn't feel right, I have to start the whole thing over. Even if... even if someone's waiting for me and I'm already running late. I just... I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Is the main reason you check the car to prevent an accident or to stop feeling anxious?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. Both, maybe. \n\nI just... like I said, before I take a ride, I have to check everything. The tires, lights, the doors... the meter. I just go around the car, over and over. Sometimes I take pictures on my phone. And if... if it doesn't feel right, I have to start all over again. Even if people are waiting. I just have to.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your main goal in checking the car is to make sure everything is safe?\n\nPatient: I guess so. Yeah. Safety. \n\nBut I mean... like I said, I just... I check it all. The tires, the meter, the doors. I take pictures of it. If it doesn't feel right, I just... I start over. Even if I'm late. I just do it.\n\nDoctor: Is the anxiety you feel when you haven't checked the car a result of thinking something bad might happen or is it more about feeling uneasy in general?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure. It's just... a bad feeling. Like something is wrong. \n\nI don't really know how to explain it. I just know I can't start driving until I... until I check everything again. It just feels wrong if I don't do it.\n\nDoctor: Is the reason you feel compelled to check the car mostly to reduce your own anxiety or mostly to prevent actual harm to others?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess I just want the bad feeling to go away. I just... I don't want anything bad to happen, either. I don't really know which one it is. I just have to do it."
